Q1. 12 workers finish a job in 10 days, working 8 hours daily. How many workers should work 6 hours daily to finish it in 16 days?
A. 19 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 12 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q2. A and B work together at first. A's time is 10 days and B's time is 15 days. If A leaves after 4 days, when will the job be finished in all?
A. 9 days
B. 13 days
C. 6 days
D. 13 1/2 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(21+14)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{70}{14}=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 9 days

---

Q3. Three workers receive ₹7200 for a job. Their contributions are proportional to 24:36:40. How much will A get?
A. ₹2073.6
B. ₹1733
C. ₹1731
D. ₹1728
Answer: ₹1728
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_wage_distribution_efficiency
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{24}{100}\times 7200
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=1728
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
S_i=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Answer: ₹1728

---

Q4. A, B and C handle documents one day at a time in rotation. They complete 10, 4 and 4 documents on their turns. In how many days will 90 documents be finished?
A. 18 days
B. 15 days
C. 14 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_group_cycle
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+4+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=n_c+s
\]
Answer: 15 days

---

Q5. A works first and B works next, then the order repeats. A completes 8 forms and B completes 6 forms on their turns. How many hours are needed for 90 forms?
A. 10 1/5 hours
B. 14 3/4 hours
C. 18 3/4 hours
D. 12 3/4 hours
Answer: 12 3/4 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=n_c+s
\]
Answer: 12 3/4 hours

---

Q6. A job can be finished in 20 days. If work continues for 5 days, what fraction is still incomplete?
A. 4/3
B. 3/1
C. 3/4
D. 1/4
Answer: 3/4
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{5}{20}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{15}{20}=3/4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{20-5}{20}=3/4
\]
Answer: 3/4

---

Q7. For 5 days, a worker finishes 15 items daily. Later the worker finishes 12 items daily. In how many days will 180 items be completed?
A. 11 3/4 days
B. 11 days
C. 17 3/4 days
D. 13 3/4 days
Answer: 13 3/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_productivity_decay
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-r_1t_1}{r_2}
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
T=5+\frac{180-15\times 5}{12}
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=13.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+t_2
\]
Answer: 13 3/4 days

---

Q8. A job is finished by 12 workers in 10 days when each works 8 hours daily. How many workers are needed for 16 days at 6 hours daily?
A. 13 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 19 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q9. A factory uses three machines. Machine A makes 20 parts per hour, B makes 25, and C makes 30. How many parts are produced in 6, 5, 4 hours respectively?
A. 364 items
B. 365 items
C. 438 items
D. 361 items
Answer: 365 items
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_machine_parallel_scheduling
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=20\times 6+25\times 5+30\times 4
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=365
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Answer: 365 items

---

Q10. 10 workers have packed 100 boxes out of 360. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 19 workers
B. 21 workers
C. 16 workers
D. 19.2 workers
Answer: 16 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{260}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-10
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=16
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 16 workers

---

Q11. A job needs 15 days of work. Work stops for 3 days during the job. How many days pass from start to finish?
A. 24 days
B. 17 days
C. 14 2/5 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{345}{8+15}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 18 days

---

Q12. A tank is filled by a pipe in 15 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 20 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?
A. 90 hours
B. 64 hours
C. 59 hours
D. 60 hours
Answer: 60 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 60 hours

---

Q13. A group is expected to complete a work in 15 days. It has already worked for 2 days. What fraction remains?
A. 15/13
B. 2/15
C. 13/2

Answer: 13/15
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:

\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{2}{15}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{13}{15}=13/15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{15-2}{15}=13/15
\]
Answer: 13/15

Q14. Water is added at 12 litres in the first hour and removed at 5 litres in the next hour, and this continues. How many hours will a 60-litre tank take to fill?
A. 12 1/4 hours
B. 21 1/3 hours
C. 15 1/3 hours
D. 16 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_spill_cycle
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

Q17. A pipe takes 12 hours to fill a tank from empty. The tank is already one-fourth full. In how many hours will it be completely filled?
A. 9 hours
B. 5 hours
C. 8 hours
D. 10 4/5 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_partial_tank_initially_filled
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{1-f}{r_f}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

Q18. 6 men and 8 women work together. A man packs 4 boxes per day and a woman packs 3 boxes per day. How many days are needed for 240 boxes?
A. 2 days
B. 5 days
C. 1/2 days
D. 6 1/4 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_group_conversion_and_or
Explanation:
Convert every worker type into a common efficiency unit.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Add the equivalent units in the working team.
\[
T=\frac{240}{6\times 4+8\times 3}
\]
Divide the total work by the team's equivalent rate.
\[
T=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 5 days

---

Q21. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 8:18. What is the ratio of the times taken by A and B for the same work?
A. 8:18
B. 26:18
C. 18:26
D. 9:4
Answer: 9:4
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
T_A:T_B=18:8
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A:T_B=9:4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=9:4
\]
Answer: 9:4

---

Q22. Pipe A fills in 12 hours, pipe B fills in 18 hours and pipe C empties in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be filled with all pipes open?
A. 9 hours
B. 15 hours
C. 10 hours
D. 11 1/4 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_two_fillers_one_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12+1/18-1/36
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 9 hours

Q23. A and B have efficiencies in the ratio 3:4. If they finish a job together in 12 days, how many days will A alone take?
A. 9 days
B. 16 days
C. 19 days
D. 28 days
Answer: 28 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W=(3+4)\times 12
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A=\frac{84}{3}=28
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(3+4)12}{3}=28
\]
Answer: 28 days

Q24. A publishing job has 120 pages. The first stages finish 36 pages. The last typist can type 6 pages per day. How many days will the last stage take?

\[

\]

\[
x=\frac{120-36}{6}
\]

\[
x=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
x=\frac{W_l}{r}
\]
Answer: 14 days

D=T_{actual}-T_{planned}

\[
R=180-12\times 6
\]

\[
D=\frac{108}{8}-\frac{108}{12}

Shortcut / Exam Method:

D=T_a-T_p

Q26. A can finish \(\frac{3}{8}\) of a work in 4 days. In how many days can A finish all of it?
A. 1 1/2 days
B. 10 2/3 days
C. 32 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 10 2/3 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_fraction_days
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=4\times \frac{8}{3}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=10\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4\div \frac{3}{8}=10\frac{2}{3}
\]
Answer: 10 2/3 days

---

Q27. A and B can separately complete a job in 10 days and 30 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 40 days
B. 20 days
C. 10 days
D. 7 1/2 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

---

Q28. In a workshop, each man finishes 4 boxes daily and each woman finishes 2 boxes daily. In how many days will 2 men and 3 women finish 300 boxes?

Answer: 16 2/3 days

Explanation:

\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Add the equivalent units in the working team.
\[
T=\frac{300}{2\times 4+3\times 2+4\times 1}
\]
Divide the total work by the team's equivalent rate.
\[
T=16\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 16 2/3 days

Q29. The efficiency ratio of A to B is 3:5. The efficiency ratio of B to C is 5:6. Find the ratio A:B:C?
A. 3:5:6:2
B. 3:5:6:3
C. 6:5:3
D. 3:5:6
Answer: 3:5:6
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:
Work with rates, not with completion times directly.
\[
B_c
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
LCM(5,5)=5
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
A:B:C=3:5:6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=3:5:6
\]
Answer: 3:5:6

Q30. A, B and C can finish a job together in 6 days. A and B together need 12 days. How many days will C alone take?
A. 19 1/5 days
B. 26 days
C. 24 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_unknown_worker_from_team
Explanation:
Work with rates, not with completion times directly.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{24}
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
T_X=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q34. A group of 24 people has food for 25 days. They consume it for 6 days, and then the group becomes 30. How many days will the remaining food last?

B. 15 1/5 days
C. 20 1/5 days
D. 11 2/5 days
Answer: 15 1/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{600-24\times 6}{30}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=15.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 15 1/5 days

Q36. A, B and C together take 8 days. A and B together take 20 days. In how many days can C alone complete it?

\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]

\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
T_X=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q37. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 36 days. For how many days will the same food last for 40 people?
A. 30 days
B. 27 days
C. 21 3/5 days
D. 31 days
Answer: 27 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
30\times 36=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=27
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{30\times 36}{40}
\]
Answer: 27 days

Q38. Food is sufficient for 20 people for 36 days. After 6 days, the number of people becomes 30. For how many more days will the food last?

Q39. For 4 days a team completes 133 forms per day. Then the team changes and completes 88 forms per day. How many days will 840 forms take altogether?

W_1=133\times 4
\]

T=4+\frac{308}{88}=7.5
\]

\]

Q40. Starting with A, two workers alternate. A completes 10 records and B completes 6 records on their respective turns. How many days will be required to complete 120 records?

Q41. A can do a job in 24 days and B in 6 days. A misses the final 2 days and B misses the final 3 days. Find the total number of days?
A. 11 2/5 days
B. 11 3/5 days
C. 7 3/5 days
D. 4 3/5 days
Answer: 7 3/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
1(T-2)+4(T-3)=24
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=7.6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 7 3/5 days

Q42. An inlet pipe can fill a tank in 12 hours. After it works for 2 hours, a leak opens and can empty the tank in 30 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 18 2/3 hours
B. 22 2/5 hours
C. 15 2/3 hours
D. 14 2/3 hours
Answer: 18 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_leak_starts_after_fill
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 2/3 hours

Q43. A tank is filled using pipes A and B. Pipe A alone takes 12 hours and pipe B alone takes 18 hours. If Pipe B is opened 2 hours later, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?

B. 12 hours
C. 9 hours
D. 6 2/5 hours
Answer: 8 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_multiple_pipes_timing
Explanation:

\[
W_1=r_At_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{30}{3+2}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Answer: 8 hours

Q44. The fraction \(\frac{2}{5}\) of a job is completed in 9 days. How many days will the whole job take?
A. 45 days
B. 3 3/5 days
C. 22 1/2 days
D. 14 days
Answer: 22 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:

\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=9\times \frac{5}{2}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=9\div \frac{2}{5}=22.5
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q45. A filling pipe fills a tank in 12 hours and a leak empties it in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled when both work together?
A. 10 hours
B. 16 hours
C. 8 hours
D. 9 hours
Answer: 10 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 10 hours

Q46. A work requires 16 workers for 12 days with 6 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 18 days with 8 hours per day?

B. 11 workers
C. 8 workers
D. 16 workers

Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
16\times 12\times 6=M_2\times 18\times 8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{16\times 12\times 6}{18\times 8}=8
\]
Answer: 8 workers

W_A:W_B=8:15
\]

\]

Q55. A can do a work in 18 days and B in 24 days. After A works for 4 days alone, both work together. In how many days is the work completed?

\]

Q57. A takes 24 days to finish a work and B takes 6 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?

Q61. The pairs A+B, B+C and C+A finish a job in 10, 12 and 15 days respectively. In how many days will A alone finish it?
A. 18 days
B. 23 days
C. 24 days
D. 29 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:
Work with rates, not with completion times directly.
\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
W_A:W_B=4:3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=4:3
\]
Answer: 4:3

Q73. A can complete a work in 12 days and B can complete it in 24 days. A starts alone, and after 3 days B joins him. In how many days will the whole work be completed?
A. 5 days
B. 13 1/2 days
C. 9 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=10\times 3,\quad R=120-30
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{90}{10+5}=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{120-10\times 3}{10+5}
\]
Answer: 9 days

Q74. Construction progresses by 12 metres daily and damage reduces it by 3 metres daily. How many days are required for 120 metres?

Q75. A and B can separately complete a job in 10 days and 15 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 6 days
B. 10 days
C. 12 1/2 days
D. 25 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,15)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/15=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+2}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 15}{10+15}=6
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q81. A work requires 15 workers for 16 days with 6 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 12 days with 8 hours per day?
A. 20 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 11 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{900-20\times 5}{25}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 32 days

Q109. Two workers A and B have efficiencies 3:4. If both together finish the job in 12 days, in how many days can A alone finish it?
A. 16 days
B. 9 days
C. 28 days
D. 19 days
Answer: 28 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_ratio_scaling
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W=(3+4)\times 12
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A=\frac{84}{3}=28
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(3+4)12}{3}=28
\]
Answer: 28 days

Q110. A+B+C finish a job in 10 days, while A+B finish the same job in 12 days. In how many days can C finish it alone?

Answer: 60 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_pairwise_total_abc
Explanation:

\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
T_X=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 60 days

Q114. A can do a job in 10 days and B in 30 days. A misses the final 2 days and B misses the final 3 days. Find the total number of days?
A. 15 3/4 days
B. 11 3/4 days
C. 9 3/4 days
D. 7 4/5 days
Answer: 9 3/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
18(T-2)+6(T-3)=180
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=9.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 3/4 days

Q115. If \(\frac{3}{8}\) of a piece of work takes 6 days, in how many days will the entire work be completed?
A. 16 days
B. 2 1/4 days
C. 14 days
D. 48 days
Answer: 16 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=6\times \frac{8}{3}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=16
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=6\div \frac{3}{8}=16
\]
Answer: 16 days

Q116. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. A drain pipe can empty the tank in 36 hours. If both are open, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 27 hours
B. 19 1/5 hours
C. 24 hours
D. 28 hours
Answer: 24 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 24 hours

Q121. 18 workers can complete a job in 8 days by working 5 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days by working 6 hours a day?

D. 13 workers

Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[
M_2=10
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q144. 10 workers have packed 80 boxes out of 240. To finish the rest in 12 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 8 workers
B. 4 workers
C. 3.2 workers
D. 7 workers
Answer: 4 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{160}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-10
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 4 workers

Q145. A team can finish a job in 12 days of actual work. The work stops for 2 days in between. In how many calendar days will the job be completed?
A. 9 days
B. 10 1/2 days
C. 14 days
D. 17 days
Answer: 14 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{96}{2+6}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+2=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 14 days

Q146. A tank is filled by a pipe in 12 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 22 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?
A. 21 2/5 hours
B. 28 2/5 hours
C. 26 2/5 hours
D. 33 hours
Answer: 26 2/5 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{22}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/5 hours

Q147. If a work is completed in 18 days and 10 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?
A. 4/9
B. 4/5
C. 5/9
D. 9/4
Answer: 4/9
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{10}{18}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{8}{18}=4/9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{18-10}{18}=4/9
\]
Answer: 4/9

Q157. A alone finishes a job in 30 days, while B alone finishes it in 18 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{90}{3+5}=11.25
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 11 1/4 days

\]

Q181. A repairs 10 metres of road on his turn and B repairs 6 metres on the next turn. They continue alternately, starting with A. If the road length is 60 metres, in how many days will the repair be completed?
A. 7 1/3 days

Answer: 7 1/3 days

Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=7\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=n_c+s
\]
Answer: 7 1/3 days

Q182. A and B can separately complete a work in 15 and 30 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 9 1/4 days
B. 12 1/3 days
C. 10 1/3 days
D. 7 1/3 days
Answer: 12 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
12(T-2)+6(T-3)=180
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=12\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 12 1/3 days

Q183. A can finish \(\frac{1}{3}\) of a work in 4 days. In how many days can A finish all of it?
A. 17 days
B. 1 1/3 days
C. 12 days
D. 7 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:

\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=4\times \frac{3}{1}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4\div \frac{1}{3}=12
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q184. Pipe A is an inlet of 12 hours and pipe B is an outlet of 36 hours. If both are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 12 hours
B. 18 hours
C. 11 hours
D. 16 hours
Answer: 12 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 12 hours

Q185. 15 workers can complete a job in 16 days by working 6 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days by working 8 hours a day?
A. 20 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 11 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q191. A works alone for 3 days on a job. A's time is 10 days and B's time is 20 days. How many days are needed in all if B joins afterward?

Answer: 7 2/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:

\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=18\times 3,\quad R=180-54
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{126}{18+9}=7\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{180-18\times 3}{18+9}
\]
Answer: 7 2/3 days

Q193. One worker can do a job in 15 days and another can do it in 30 days. In how many days will they complete it together?

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[
T=\frac{30}{2+1}=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]

Answer: 7/12

Q217. 18 workers working 5 hours per day finish the job in 8 days. If the daily working time is 6 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days?
A. 18 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 27 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q218. 48 of 180 forms are complete. 8 workers are available and 8 days remain. How many additional workers are required?

\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]

\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{132}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 9 workers

Q219. A job would be completed in 12 days of work. Due to a 3 days stoppage, in how many calendar days is it completed?
A. 11 days
B. 12 days
C. 15 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{240}{15+5}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q220. Without leakage, a tank fills in 10 hours. With leakage, it fills in 16 hours. Find the leak's emptying time in hours?
A. 30 2/3 hours
B. 40 hours
C. 26 2/3 hours
D. 23 2/3 hours
Answer: 26 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{16}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/3 hours

Q221. A team can complete a job in 24 days. If it works for 10 days, what fraction of the job will still be unfinished?

\[
R=1-\frac{10}{24}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.

\]

\[
R=\frac{24-10}{24}=7/12
\]
Answer: 7/12

Q234. A repairs 10 metres of road on his turn and B repairs 4 metres on the next turn. They continue alternately, starting with A. If the road length is 90 metres, in how many days will the repair be completed?

Q235. A and B work on a job but leave before it is completed. A leaves 2 days early and B leaves 3 days early. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 10 days. How long does the job take?
A. 8 days
B. 11 days
C. 10 days
D. 14 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
20(T-2)+24(T-3)=240
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q252. A can do a work in 12 days and B in 6 days. After A works for 2 days alone, both work together. In how many days is the work completed?

C. 5 1/3 days
D. 7 1/3 days
Answer: 5 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{50}{5+10}=5\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{60-5\times 2}{5+10}
\]
Answer: 5 1/3 days

Q253. A team repairs 12 metres daily, but another effect damages 4 metres daily. How many days are needed to complete 120 metres?
A. 20 days
B. 11 1/4 days
C. 15 days
D. 17 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]

\[
T=\frac{120}{12-4}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 15 days

Q254. A alone finishes a job in 8 days, while B alone finishes it in 24 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 8 days
B. 6 days
C. 32 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+1}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 24}{8+24}=6
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q255. A needs 20 days for a work and A with B needs 12 days. In how many days can B do the work alone?
A. 8 days
B. 32 days
C. 30 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 30 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:

\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{12\times 20}{20-12}=30
\]
Answer: 30 days

Q256. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 12 days and B alone needs 6 days. A leaves after 2 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?
A. 2 days

C. 5 days
D. 6 1/4 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:

\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(5+10)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{30}{10}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 5 days

Q259. A team takes 20 days for a job. After working for 8 days, what fraction of the job remains?
A. 5/3
B. 2/5
C. 3/5
D. 3/2
Answer: 3/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{8}{20}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{12}{20}=3/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{20-8}{20}=3/5
\]
Answer: 3/5

Q260. 10 workers have completed 60 of 360 forms. Only 12 days are left. How many extra workers are needed to finish on time?
A. 18 workers
B. 20 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 15 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{300}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-10
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q291. If a work is completed in 24 days and 4 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?
A. 5/6
B. 6/5
C. 5/1
D. 1/6
Answer: 5/6
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{4}{24}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{20}{24}=5/6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{24-4}{24}=5/6
\]
Answer: 5/6

Q292. A team of 12 workers has completed 72 items from a 240-item order. With 10 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 6 workers
B. 1 worker
C. 7.5 workers
D. 5 workers
Answer: 5 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{168}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 5 workers

Q293. A group needs 10 working days to complete a job. If there is a break of 2 days, how many days pass from start to finish?
A. 14 2/5 days
B. 8 days
C. 12 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{640}{40+24}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+2=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q294. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 15 hours, but with leakage it fills in 25 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 40 1/2 hours
B. 37 1/2 hours
C. 56 1/4 hours
D. 33 1/2 hours
Answer: 37 1/2 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{25}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=37.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 37 1/2 hours

Q310. A alone finishes a job in 20 days, while B alone finishes it in 12 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

\[

\]

\[

\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{3+5}=7.5

\]

D=10.5

\]

Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{18}
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
B=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{10\times 18}{18-10}=22.5
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q326. A and B start the same job. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 16 days. After 5 days only B continues. How many days are needed in all?
A. 8 days
B. 9 3/5 days
C. 6 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]

\[
W_1=(8+5)\times 5

\[

\]

Q330. In a joint job, A alone needs 10 days and B alone needs 20 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?

D. 12 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=9
\]

\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 days

Q344. A job has 180 forms. 8 workers have finished 48 forms, and 12 days remain. How many more workers should be added?

B. 3.75 workers
C. 5 workers
D. 0.5 workers
Answer: 3 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{132}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 3 workers

Q364. A repair team completes 18 metres per day and daily damage removes 3 metres. In how many days will 180 metres be completed?

D. 14 days
Answer: 12 days

\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Apply the active rates for their respective times.
\[
T=\frac{180}{18-3}
\]
Use the resulting work equation to compute the asked quantity.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q366. A and B complete the same work separately in 10 and 30 days. What is the ratio of work done by A and B in one day?

D. 10:30
Answer: 3:1

Explanation:

\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{10}:\frac{1}{30}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W_A:W_B=30:10
\]

\[
W_A:W_B=3:1
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:1
\]
Answer: 3:1

Q367. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 30 days. A works alone for 2 days, then B joins. How many days will the job take in all?
A. 8 days
B. 10 days
C. 3 days
D. 4 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=18\times 2,\quad R=180-36
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{144}{18+6}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{180-18\times 2}{18+6}
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q369. A and B can separately complete a job in 6 days and 10 days. What is the time taken when they work together?

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\]
Answer: 3 3/4 days

Q373. A group is expected to complete a work in 30 days. It has already worked for 10 days. What fraction remains?
A. 1/3
B. 2/3
C. 2/1
D. 3/2
Answer: 2/3
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{10}{30}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{20}{30}=2/3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{30-10}{30}=2/3
\]
Answer: 2/3

Q374. 12 workers have completed 120 of 240 forms. Only 8 days are left. How many extra workers are needed to finish on time?
A. 6 workers
B. 0.5 workers
C. 4.5 workers
D. 3 workers
Answer: 3 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{120}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 3 workers

\]

\[

\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 9 days

\[

\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{2+1}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 24}{12+24}=8
\]
Answer: 8 days

---

Q399. 20 people have food for 30 days. If the number of people becomes 40, for how many days will the food last?
A. 15 days
B. 12 days
C. 19 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 30=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 30}{40}
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q401. A does \(\frac{4}{9}\) of a job in 9 days. What is the time required for the complete job?

Q402. If A's time is 18 days and B's time is 6 days, what ratio of work will they do in equal time?
A. 6:24
B. 18:6
C. 1:3
D. 24:6
Answer: 1:3
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{18}:\frac{1}{6}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W_A:W_B=6:18
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
W_A:W_B=1:3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=1:3
\]
Answer: 1:3

Q403. A begins a job alone. A can finish it in 15 days and B can finish it in 30 days. If B joins after 3 days, how many days will the job take in all?
A. 8 1/4 days
B. 9 days
C. 6 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 11 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=8\times 3,\quad R=120-24
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{96}{8+4}=11
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{120-8\times 3}{8+4}
\]
Answer: 11 days

---

Q404. A team adds 18 metres to a project each day, but 3 metres are lost each day. What is the time for 180 metres?
A. 15 days
B. 16 days
C. 12 days
D. 9 3/5 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{180}{18-3}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q405. A alone finishes a job in 10 days, while B alone finishes it in 30 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 20 days
B. 40 days
C. 7 1/2 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

Q406. A can finish a work in 20 days and B in 18 days. They work together for 5 days, then B alone finishes it. How many days are taken altogether?
A. 16 1/2 days
B. 9 1/2 days
C. 20 1/4 days
D. 13 1/2 days
Answer: 13 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(18+20)\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{170}{20}=13.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 13 1/2 days

Q407. Starting with A, two workers alternate. A completes 8 records and B completes 6 records on their respective turns. How many hours will be required to complete 120 records?
A. 20 hours
B. 25 1/2 hours
C. 13 hours
D. 17 hours
Answer: 17 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=17
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=n_c+s
\]
Answer: 17 hours

Q408. A group is expected to complete a work in 10 days. It has already worked for 8 days. What fraction remains?
A. 1/5
B. 1/4
C. 4/5
D. 5/1
Answer: 1/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{8}{10}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{2}{10}=1/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{10-8}{10}=1/5
\]
Answer: 1/5

---

Q409. 8 workers have completed 80 of 240 forms. Only 10 days are left. How many extra workers are needed to finish on time?
A. 3 workers
B. 6 workers
C. 4 workers
D. 8 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{160}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 8 workers

---

Q435. A does \(\frac{2}{5}\) of a job in 8 days. What is the time required for the complete job?
A. 20 days
B. 3 1/5 days
C. 40 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_fraction_days
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=8\times \frac{5}{2}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=8\div \frac{2}{5}=20
\]
Answer: 20 days

Q436. A and B can separately complete a job in 12 days and 6 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 18 days
B. 9 days
C. 4 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,6)=12
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=12/12=1,\quad B=12/6=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{12}{1+2}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 6}{12+6}=4
\]
Answer: 4 days

---

Q437. There is enough food for 30 people for 24 days. What is its duration for 25 people?
A. 43 1/5 days
B. 25 4/5 days
C. 32 4/5 days
D. 28 4/5 days
Answer: 28 4/5 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
30\times 24=25\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=28.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{30\times 24}{25}
\]
Answer: 28 4/5 days

Q438. A camp has food for 20 people for 45 days. After 6 days, the camp has 30 people. For how many more days will the remaining food last?

\]

\[
D=\frac{900-20\times 6}{30}

\]
Answer: 26 days

Q440. A can complete a work in 24 days and B can complete it in 10 days. In the same number of days, what is the ratio of the work done by A and B?
A. 34:10
B. 5:12
C. 10:34
D. 24:10
Answer: 5:12
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{24}:\frac{1}{10}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W_A:W_B=10:24
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
W_A:W_B=5:12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=5:12
\]
Answer: 5:12

Q441. A alone finishes a job in 12 days, while B alone finishes it in 6 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

Q442. If a work is completed in 10 days and 2 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?
A. 5/4
B. 4/5
C. 4/1
D. 1/5
Answer: 4/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{2}{10}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{8}{10}=4/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{10-2}{10}=4/5
\]
Answer: 4/5

---

Q443. A team of 10 workers has completed 80 items from a 240-item order. With 10 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 4.8 workers
B. 7 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 6 workers
Answer: 6 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{160}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-10
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 6 workers

---

Q444. A team can finish a job in 15 days of actual work. The work stops for 1 day in between. In how many calendar days will the job be completed?
A. 19 1/5 days
B. 16 days
C. 21 days
D. 19 days
Answer: 16 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{210}{10+4}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+1=16
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 16 days

---

\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{240}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 18 workers

---

Q472. A job would be completed in 8 days of work. Due to a 3 days stoppage, in how many calendar days is it completed?
A. 7 days
B. 8 days
C. 13 1/5 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 11 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{448}{20+36}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=11
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 11 days

---

Q473. A team can complete a job in 10 days. If it works for 6 days, what fraction of the job will still be unfinished?
A. 5/2
B. 3/5
C. 2/3
D. 2/5
Answer: 2/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{6}{10}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{4}{10}=2/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{10-6}{10}=2/5
\]
Answer: 2/5

Q475. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 20:8. What is the ratio of the times taken by A and B for the same work?

B. 8:28
C. 28:8
D. 20:8
Answer: 2:5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:

\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
T_A:T_B=8:20
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A:T_B=2:5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2:5
\]
Answer: 2:5

Q476. If \(\frac{3}{8}\) of a piece of work takes 12 days, in how many days will the entire work be completed?
A. 96 days
B. 20 days
C. 32 days
D. 4 1/2 days
Answer: 32 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_fraction_days
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=12\times \frac{8}{3}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=12\div \frac{3}{8}=32
\]
Answer: 32 days

Q477. A can complete a piece of work in 6 days and B can complete the same work in 12 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 4 days
B. 6 days
C. 18 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,12)=12
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=12/6=2,\quad B=12/12=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{12}{2+1}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 12}{6+12}=4
\]
Answer: 4 days

Q485. Food is sufficient for 20 people for 24 days. For how many days will the same food last for 40 people?
A. 9 days
B. 12 days
C. 13 days
D. 7 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 24=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 24}{40}
\]
Answer: 12 days
